[[Applause]] [[The Gathered Rise Up]] The resolution of the Polish Sejm on support and equalisation of people in the spectrum of autism. The Sejm of the Republic of Poland, bearing in mind the needs of more than one million citizens - people in the spectrum of autism, their families and carers, recognizes that the state should create them decent living and working conditions. In order to make this possible, the Polish Parliament supports system solutions to create a comprehensive policy aimed at all these people. Taking into account the obligations arising from resolutions: Charter of the Rights of Persons with Autism and the Charter of the Rights of Persons with Disabilities, the Sejm of the Republic of Poland considers it important to implement the strategy of eliminating mental, organisational and financial barriers by developing the current system of care for persons in the spectrum of autism, so as to implement their rights in all areas of life taking into account the level of their functioning. Each person in the spectrum of autism should be allowed to function as dignified and as independent as possible in adulthood, with particular regard to inclusive education, equal opportunities in the labour market and a modern care and support system. The Sejm of the Republic of Poland, stating that the above obligation stems from the Constitution of the Republic of Poland, the Universal Declaration of Human Rights, the Convention on the Rights of Persons with Disabilities, the Convention on the Rights of the Child, the Standard Rules of Equalisation of the Opportunities of Persons with Disabilities and other acts of international and internal law, supports all actions aimed at developing the strategy. Please allow us to adopt this resolution by acclamation. [[Applause]] Thank you very much. I ask the Secretary to read the messages.

ParlaMint-PL_2021-04-14-sejm-28-1.u13	Dear Marshal! High Izbo! Since 1 July 2020, the head of the National Tax Administration has taken over the task of collecting tolls and is carrying out work to implement a modern electronic toll collection system for heavy vehicles based on satellite positioning technology in Poland. This system will replace the currently used viaTOLL system. The bill submitted to the High House will form the basis for the creation of a uniform collection system for all road tolls. We aim primarily at the benefit of citizens: the proposed project will allow us to move away from the existing method of charging tolls based on motorway gates. Currently, the head of the KAS charges a toll on two sections of toll motorways: A2 - Konin - Stryków, A4 - Bielany Wrocławskie - Sośnica. It was on these episodes in the first place on 1 December this year. a new method for collecting tolls by highway will be raised and a new method for collecting tolls will be implemented. Such time limit shall be linked to the expiry of the existing gate service contracts. The extension of these agreements would require a multi-million-dollar expenditure on their modernisation, maintenance, replacement of equipment... [[Game in the O.R.]] [[Piotr Zgorzelski, Vice-Marshal of the Sejm, is the chairman of the proceedings.]] Excuse me, Minister. May I ask you to allow the Minister to speak? Excuse me, just a second. There you go. Secretary of State at the Ministry of Finance Magdalena Rzeczkowska: Thank you. The project provides for two ways to pay for motorway travel: by purchasing a motorway ticket and based on geolocation data, as is the case with heavy vehicles. A freeway ticket will be available for purchase not only through modern online distribution channels, but also at fuel stations, in the kiosk. This will be convenient for people who do not use digital solutions. We don't forget people like that. The project does not require the storage, transmission of geolocation data or installation of applications, and I would like to emphasise that. It is on the citizens' side to choose the form of payment of the fee. If you buy a motorway ticket, the National Tax Administration will not know the driver's identity. The proposed rules also introduce the possibility of changing the method of charging on the concession sections of the A1, A2 and A4 motorways. The need to harmonise the toll collection system also on these sections was indicated by the Supreme Audit Chamber and the draft implements the recommendations of the Chamber. Such a change will be possible upon request, with the consent of the concession holders, without prejudice to the obligations of the State Treasury arising from agreements already concluded. Even if the concession holders do not decide to change the method of charging, the ticket solution increases interoperability, the convenience of citizens. Allowing entrepreneurs to distribute motorway tickets will allow them to use the same tools, e.g. mobile applications have been purchased a ticket on the state highway, but it is also about paying a fee on the concession section. The obligation to pay a toll on the motorway will rest on the owner, the owner, the vehicle user. Similarly to public transport tickets or parking charges, the non-payment of the fee will result in the imposition of an additional charge. The fee will not be charged if an electronic ticket for the unpaid journey is purchased within 3 days of the end of the journey. I would also stress that the proposed project provides for easier registration of carriers in the e-TOLL system. We reduce the scope of data that will be necessary for registration. Above all, we abolish the obligation to provide registration numbers for trailers and semi-trailers. The proposed rules will also allow part of the obligations related to the payment of road tolls by the fleet card provider. It's gonna be like this. registration or update of data. Similarly, as the distributor of motorway tickets, the fleet card provider will conclude a contract with the head of the KAS. This agreement will be open to any entity providing such services under uniform conditions. When building an e-TOLL system, the National Tax Administration engages entrepreneurs in order to provide convenient service to citizens. The provisions also provide for arrangements to maintain the integrity of the toll collection system by ensuring that the correct payment of tolls by all users is checked. These checks will be carried out by Customs officers, supplementing police officers and road transport inspection inspectors. The verification of the correct payment of tolls by toll sections of motorways will also be carried out on the basis of the data provided from the camera system installed on these sections. The benefits of the planned concept of highway tolling are primarily to reduce traffic congestion by not having to stop vehicles before gates in order to pay tolls, and to be open to interoperability at national level in cooperation with concession holders. [[♪ The bell ♪]] It will be possible to include a uniform toll collection system for motorways of concessions. This means that there is no need to bear the costs of expanding and maintaining toll stations and motorway gates, improving the comfort of drivers due to reduced travel times, introducing convenient methods of payment for toll motorways and facilitating for professional carriers. Thank you very much and I ask the High House to consider the bill. [[Applause]]
ParlaMint-PL_2021-04-14-sejm-28-1.u14	Thank you very much, Minister. The Sejm agreed that in the discussion on this item it would hear five minutes' statements on behalf of clubs and circles. ParlaMint-PL_2021-04-14-sejm-28-1.u15	Mr. Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! On behalf of the Parliamentary Club, Law and Justice, I have the honour to present my position on Draft Print Act No. 1073. The adoption of the proposed law will primarily serve toll road users and will allow the elimination of gates at the beginning of the motorway sections: Konin - Stryków, this is the A2 motorway and Wrocław - Sośnica, the A4 motorway, from 1 December 2021. The movement will be able to proceed smoothly, without stopping before the so-called. grounded. The scope of the required data for registration in the e-TOLL system will be reduced. A single electronic toll collection system for heavy and light vehicles will be introduced. It also introduces the possibility to pay a toll for the motorway by purchasing an electronic motorway ticket or on the basis of geolocation data thanks to the free e-TOLL.pl application. The user of such a ticket will determine the beginning and end of the journey on toll motorway sections and the start of the journey. A one-time freeway ticket will be valid for 48 hours on a specific section of the highway. The toll detection record will be verified in the SPOE control system, i.e. the SPOE KAS, the National Tax Administration electronic toll collection system, and the ticket assigned to the vehicle will not be used again. The purchased ticket will be refundable before the declared date and time of departure. During the motorway journey, if you change your travel plan, you will be able to purchase an additional ticket, e.g. at the place of service to travellers or at the gas station. Providing vehicle users with such wide opportunities to purchase a motorway ticket, not only in modern online distribution channels, e.g. on websites, self-service terminals and mobile apps provided by external companies, but also in the form of a printed ticket purchased at a gas station or kiosk, it will ensure that people who do not benefit from digital solutions can pay the fee. Some of the benefits of the presented project: eliminating the congestion at the current toll collection points, avoiding the need for complex and costly integration of systems, SPE KAS with MSPO, i.e. the System for the Collection of Electronic Charges of National Tax Administration with manual toll collection system, reducing the high cost of maintenance of MSPO, introducing convenient payment methods and shortening travel times, reducing the cost of maintenance of the serviced MSPO infrastructure. The legislation currently under discussion by the High Sejm will make it easier for users of public roads and will at the same time be a source of savings both for users and for public finances. In view of the above, Parliamentary Club Law and Justice will vote in favour of the bill in its entirety. Thank you. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2021-04-14-sejm-28-1.u21	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! On behalf of the parliamentary club of the Left, I have the honour to present to the High House a position on the government draft law on toll motorways and on the National Road Fund and on certain other laws. We are proceeding in an express pace over a law which, together with the justification, counts several dozen pages, a law in which there are no expert opinions. According to the reasons submitted, the Act aims to increase capacity and reduce congestion on toll motorways. It's about Konin - Stryków and Wrocław - Sośnica episodes. The gates will disappear and the highways will be grounded. The exhaust emissions will decrease. The new method of charging will operate within the KAS e-TOLL electronic toll collection system. It is intended to replace the existing viaTOLL system. The driver will need to register in the system, install and run the OBU - an external location system - and a free e-TOLL mobile application. It will also be possible to purchase a ticket on-line or in print form. The introduction of charges based on geolocation data using the application has the advantage that it does not cause delays. It also has a flaw: such a way of making payments does not seem to be used today on any section of motorways. According to the government, the advantages of the proposed solutions are: allowing for smooth highway rides, the lack of cost of maintaining infrastructure for charging, the release of people. There are also downsides: before entering the toll highway section you will need to run the application and it will not be allowed to turn off until you leave the road. If the app breaks down, it suspends, we're gonna have to head to the nearest exit. We will be able to continue the journey only after the payment of the fee has been completed, e.g. after you recharge your phone. It's about buying a one-time e-ticket. If the charge is carried out in a geolocation system and when driving on the motorway of the driver you will unload your phone and will not be able to charge it, there will be an additional penalty of 500 PLN, unless within 3 days you pay the toll. If a car uses several people in the family, each of them has to download an application. The integrity of the toll collection system will be controlled by the Customs-Scarb Service, the Road Transport Inspection and the Police. The project also offers the possibility of eliminating toll collection points and gates on concession sections. In this case, the concession holders will have to give up their own fees at their own request, which, of course, involves changing existing contracts. The point is to transfer the liability for the collection of fees to the KAS and receive compensation for the lost benefits. So you're gonna take over the tolling obligation and pay the concession holders to drive cars on their highways. The Road Transport Forum, which brings together dozens of economic organisations as regards transport companies, raises serious doubts about this law and how it will be implemented. In particular, it points to a lack of consultation. It also proposes that the system's assumptions be modified. The aim is to provide road users with unlimited access to the free on-board OBU device, which would be made available on bail by the system operator, as a basic form of payment of electronic tolls for the use of toll roads in Poland. The Forum also calls for a transitional period - from 6 to 12 months - during which road toll will be suspended from heavy goods vehicles and buses. This is the period from the switch-off of the viaTOLL system to be completed in June 2021 until it is fully prepared to start and test the e-TOLL system. This includes the admission to EETS and fleet cards systems. Other demands are: to modify the rules for imposing penalties for unpaid tolls in such a way that before they are imposed e-TOLL users are called upon to pay unpaid charges, to waive the planned annual increase in road charges by an inflation rate - this is not linked to the actual cost of maintaining and developing the road network - and to carry out in good time a reliable information campaign on the new e-TOLL toll collection system addressed to system users. The Act requires employers to equip their cars with equipment. The government doesn't care that they have to buy 0.5 million smartphones, 0.5 million devices. Drivers won't use their own phones to turn on the app. You gave me the app, and you're worried, man. I would like to ask: Who will produce such a large number of devices in such a short time? Who's gonna pay for it? You've wasted your time, and today you want to make up for it. You're eliminating one system, you're introducing another system, and you're trying to pass the bill on to road users. Why do you not want to equip entrepreneurs with these devices according to the directive? I will answer: because the ministry will not be held responsible for the fact that they do not operate and that entrepreneurs will be punished. ParlaMint-PL_2021-04-14-sejm-28-1.u171	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! I have the honour to present the position of the Parliamentary Club Law and Justice towards the government draft law amending the Law on the greenhouse gas emissions trading scheme and certain other laws contained in print No 1008. This project complements the implementation of the EU ETS Directive, namely Directive 2003/87/EC, on a number of technical issues and its main issue is the implementation of the Modernisation Fund regulation into national law. The task of this new instrument is to support the achievement of energy and climate policy objectives by supporting the modernisation of energy systems in selected countries of the European Union. Poland is to be its biggest beneficiary. The Modernization Fund is powered by 2% of resources the pool of all allowances. The estimated share of Poland is up to PLN 20 billion in 2021-2030. The objectives for which funds can be allocated from the Modernisation Fund include the production and use of electricity from renewable energy sources, heating, energy storage and modernisation of energy networks, including pipelines belonging to heating systems and electricity grids. This includes the transformation of regions dependent on solid fossil fuels and the promotion of fair change and the financing of energy efficiency, including transport, construction, agriculture and waste. Another element of this project is the changes that allow the allocation of free emission allowances to entities that have been covered by the ETS. Once approved by the European Commission, it will be possible to adjust the number of emission allowances by the National Emission Balancing and Management Centre and to issue emission allowances to installation accounts in the Union registry on the basis of changes in levels of activity. The project provides that the management of funds from the Fund will be entrusted to the National Fund for Environmental Protection and Water Management and that the management of the system will be carried out by the Minister responsible for Climate Affairs. According to the project, the financing of investments modernising our energy system and improving energy efficiency will take place on the basis of priority programmes. High Izbo! The adoption of the Act is important for the legal stabilisation of installations covered by the ETS. It will contribute to modernising the energy system, to achieving the objectives set out in the energy policy adopted by the government, to reducing greenhouse gas emissions and to improving air quality. Thus, it will significantly improve the quality of life of Poles. Moreover, these investments will also have a positive impact on the economy, creating jobs and improving the competitiveness of Polish companies in the international arena. Therefore, on behalf of the Parliamentary Club, I request that the proposed bill be adopted together with the amendments adopted by the relevant Parliamentary Committee and that it be further examined. Thank you. [[Applause]]

The Emissions Trading Directive states that at least half of these resources should be earmarked for specific purposes. That's for example. reduction of greenhouse gas emissions, development of RES, promotion of energy efficiency. It is worth noting that one of the most important changes which, after the adoption of the law, will improve the protection of identity cards against counterfeiting, is the introduction of biometrics in the electronic layer in the form of fingerprints. It is also proposed to amend the period of validity of personal evidence. It will be 5 years, and as a rule 10 years, while in an emergency - 1 year. A change in the graphic layer shall also be made where the signature of the holder of the identity card will be entered. All this is to increase safety. I think it is also worth mentioning that this way we meet the safety requirements of international civil aviation. That, of course, also matters. The draft law also affects an area such as the way in which an identity card is requested, transfers the identification of a model identity card from the regulation - as has been the case so far - to the law. Finally, the proposed law also changes the conditions for the exchange of evidence by introducing inter alia. a statement stating that the change in the name of the place of birth will not necessitate the exchange of the document. ParlaMint-PL_2021-04-14-sejm-28-1.u230	Great. Thanks a lot. Mr Wiesław Szczepański, parliamentary club of the Left.
ParlaMint-PL_2021-04-14-sejm-28-1.u231	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! Minister! On behalf of the parliamentary club of the Left, I have the honour to present our position to the High House regarding the government draft law amending the Act on Personal Evidence and some other laws. Yesterday, the Committee on Administration and Home Affairs almost unanimously examined this proposal at first reading and almost unanimously recommended its adoption to the High House. According to the Act, another biometric feature will be introduced into the ID: fingerprints. Their owners' signatures will appear again on the I.D. The new rules are due to enter into force on 2 August this year. and adapt the Polish right to current EU regulations, namely the 2019 European Union and Council of Europe Regulation of 20 June on the security of personal evidence of citizens of the European Union and residence permits issued to citizens of the Union and their family members enjoying the right to free movement. The purpose of the Regulation is to reduce the risk of counterfeiting of documents and offences against the reliability of documents by removing the significant differences in the level of safeguards of national documents for the exercise of the right of free movement. This Regulation imposes new obligations on the European Union countries concerning the necessary safeguards in the identity documents, including, inter alia, the obligation to introduce a second biometric characteristic, i.e. fingerprints. It is also about the need to adapt the identity card to the specifications and minimum safety standards. Fingerprints will be taken when submitting an application for identity cards in the municipality. The Act also specifies the maximum period for the storage of fingerprints in the Register of Personal Evidence, i.e. until the date of receipt of the identity card, not more than 90 days from the date of issue, i.e. personalisation of proof. If it is not possible from the applicant for ID to collect his fingerprints, e.g. Because of the illness, this ID will only be valid for 12 months. No fingerprints will also be admissible for children up to 12 years of age. He's also returning to the I.D., as I said, the signature of the owner of the document. The amendments to the Act also apply to the term of validity of personal evidence. In the case of personal evidence of children under 12 years of age, the proof will be valid for a period of 5 years. For people who are over 12 years of age, this will be a standard period of 10 years. As fingerprints will be collected from the applicant, the application for an identity card of a person over the age of 12 will only be made in person in the municipality. This will also require the training of municipal staff and the equipment of offices for fingerprinting devices. The current identity cards shall remain valid depending on their date of issue and shall be replaced by 2030. Due to the increased expenditure of the Ministry of Internal Affairs and Administration and municipalities, the government plans additionally several hundred million PLN spread over 10 years to carry out this task. The government also assured the committee that it would be ready from the beginning of June - he and the municipalities - to carry out this task. Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! Minister! At the first reading, my club at the committee meeting had tabled an amendment to ensure that the identity card issued to a person who was 70 years of age was valid for an unlimited period of time from the date on which it was issued. This entry is in line with the suggestion in Article. Point 4 (2) of the 2019 Regulation of the European Parliament and of the Council of Europe Since this amendment did not obtain a majority, we reported it as a minority request. I am asking the Minister and, as I see, the Director present here, that you review this and agree to accept this solution. My club will vote for this bill. Mr. Marshal, I've saved you a minute. In May, training will also begin as regards the local government side. As far as the tender process is concerned, the State asked, and the purchase of scanners, it is taking time and everything will be realized at the moment. At the moment, there are no risks regarding the implementation of the second biometric feature in personal evidence. Ladies and gentlemen! To answer the question about whether the evidence could be valid for more than 10 years for people over the age of 70, I would say that we would like it to be. This was the case in Poland until the proof had no electronic version. Today's proof is not only a blank, but there is also an electronic version. As you know, on all issues relating to computerisation, it is very important that there are certificates that ensure the security of the electronic version. Many of our compatriots, including during the pandemic, are increasingly using different e-services. We want to expand the electronic version and the functionality of personal evidence and we need to ensure security. These certificates are up to 10 years of validity in terms of safety, so if we wanted to extend the validity of the proof, it would not be possible to secure the electronic version to Poles at the level at which we would like it to be. So, I answer the question - today there is no possibility to issue proof for unlimited time and there will be no further possibility. That's why the government went against it. The point is that there is no such situation that someone will have proof, but it will not be safe from the electronic layer. You have often asked about the issues of local governments. I would like to point out that the joint committee of the government and the local government adopted the proposal without any comments, i.e. the self-government party has not submitted any comments. He's prepared for the expense side, ladies and gentlemen. The grant for the task shall be awarded on the basis of the number of tasks carried out. The amount of the grant is a man-hour rate of 31 PLN. There is also an increased time-consuming task due to the fact that there will be fingerprinting - from 30 minutes to 37. A new task will be assigned: issuing an ID card outside the office. The intensity of this action is, of course, much greater - 2 hours. The increase in grant costs only this year by PLN 6 million in the perspective of 10 years is an increase in grant by PLN 160 million for local governments. There was a question about the whole (Bingle) expenses. That's about PLN 363 million. If you don't mind, Mr. Marshal, I'll answer two more frequently asked questions. As far as the maximum period of validity in the European Union is concerned, this maximum period of validity is, in the vast majority, 10 years for personal evidence. When it comes to holding a second biometric characteristic, i.e. fingerprints, it is no longer than 90 days in the Register of Personal Evidence. No EU authorities, as has been said here, will hold the I.D. data. Only when crossing borders, as it is today, does the border guard of the other country have an insight into this data at the time of crossing, and that will continue to be the case. The proof will be issued as it is today's passport. To apply for a passport, you have to make fingerprints. To pick up a passport, you have to make fingerprints, too. It's the security of public documents. And the last question that was: Why can't you submit online? That's why, ladies and gentlemen, there's no possibility of a large-scale online fingerprinting today. You just have to go to the office and file an application at the office. Thank you very much for those questions that were relevant. I hope that you have also received a substantive answer. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-PL_2021-04-14-sejm-28-1.u264	Thank you very much. The Sejm agreed that in the discussion on this item it would hear five minutes' statements on behalf of clubs and circles. I'm opening a discussion. Mr Jacek World on behalf of the Parliamentary Club Law and Justice. You're welcome, sir.
ParlaMint-PL_2021-04-14-sejm-28-1.u265	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! I present the position of the Parliamentary Club Law and Justice on the draft law amending the Act on the Protection of Monuments and the Protection of Monuments and the Act on National Tax Administration. Let me remind you that on 17 April 2019 a Regulation of the European Parliament and of the Council on the introduction and import of cultural goods was adopted. We are obliged to introduce criminal rules into our legal order for the operation of this regulation. Anyway, it's urgent because the deadline is actually over. The purpose of the Regulation is to adopt common rules in the European Union to protect against the illicit trade in cultural goods imported from third countries and to prevent the financing of terrorism by the sale of illegally acquired and illegally disposed goods. The problem isn't trivial. Large-scale artifacts from plunder or illegal excavations are exported from countries under war, terrorism, or without stable power, such as Syria or Iraq. The sale of cultural monuments is today the third source of terrorist financing after human trafficking and oil trafficking. This is even greater than drug trafficking. The Regulation of the European Parliament and of the Council aims to put an end to this practice. Our role is important in this respect, because Poland protects a large part of the external borders of the Union. Today we are a transit country and maybe not the main one, but the route of smuggling cultural goods. We must not allow Poland to become a weak link in the protection of the customs territory of the European Union. This Act is primarily intended to give the National Tax Administration more effective tools to combat this type of crime. The Act therefore provides for adequate penalties - including prison penalties - for the illegal import and circulation of cultural objects and the possibility of confiscation of such objects. The Annex to the EU Regulation contains a list of prohibited goods is referred to here. During the meeting of the cultural committee there was an interesting discussion about the restitution of Polish cultural goods located outside the eastern borders. This subject, of course, will become, I hope, as Minister Gawin promises, the subject of a separate committee debate, but of course there is no direct link between the contested law and this discussion. The Law and Justice Club is, of course, in favour of the whole bill.
ParlaMint-PL_2021-04-14-sejm-28-1.u266	Thank you very much. Mr Wojciech Król, Civic Coalition. You're welcome.
ParlaMint-PL_2021-04-14-sejm-28-1.u267	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! The draft Act amending the Act on the Protection of Monuments and the Protection of Monuments and the Act on National Tax Administration aims to implement the provisions of the Regulation of the European Parliament which prohibits the introduction of cultural objects into the European Union if they have been illegally removed from the territory of the State in which they were created or discovered. Pursuant to that Regulation, Member States shall lay down the rules on penalties applicable to infringements of the Regulation and shall take all necessary measures to ensure that they are implemented. The essence of the solutions contained in the draft is the penalization of illegal entry, which will result in the sentence of imprisonment of the perpetrators up to the age of 2. The draft also provides for the amendment of the Act of 16 November 2016 on National Tax Administration and the extension of the powers and powers of the heads of customs offices for detection, recognition and, in particular, the fight against the offence in question. At the same time, it will be possible for the court to decide on the forfeiture of the cultural goods which came from the crime. Attention should be paid to the scale of the global art market, the heritage of culture, which is one of the important sources of funding for criminal groups, money laundering or terrorist financing. The scale of the operation shall be signalled by the special services, as well as by the services specialising in the fight against crimes arising from trade in stolen works of art. To sum up, the proposed amendment to the Act on the Protection of Monuments and the Protection of Monuments, as well as the Act on the National Tax Administration of the Civic Coalition, which I represent, will support. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2021-04-14-sejm-28-1.u299	Mr. Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! We are proceeding with the bill amending the Act - Code of Civil Procedure and some other laws, prints no. 899 and 1034. The aim of the project is to clarify civil proceedings during and one year after the pandemic. The purpose of the Act is to safeguard courts against exposure not only to workers but also to all participants in judicial proceedings. Identifying the case in this way, i.e. by means of a distance agreement, it complies with the principle of transparency and at the same time eliminates the epidemiological risk. Persons taking part in proceedings may stay at home, in a law firm or elsewhere if the court is serious. I would like to stress - it is very important - that without the permission of the court there will be no room in another procedure. Let me remind you that the bill regulates, among others. solutions for auctions from movable and immovable property. The above is a very positive interest from those involved in auctions and the media. The tender as part of the sale of real estate would be carried out in the electronic system. At present, there is no regulation enabling the tender to be conducted in such a way. A request for an electronic tender could be made both within the first and the second bidding dates. In the proposed digital version of the tender, a document describing and estimating the value of the property will be included in the electronic system operating the tender and will be available to a wide range of buyers interested in e-booking. The electronic tender is to last a week, following the example of functioning electronic auctions. In the European Union, court auctions are conducted online: Austria in cooperation with Germany, Croatia, Estonia, Finland, Spain, Latvia - only in case of real estate enforcement proceedings - Germany in cooperation with Austria, the Netherlands - only in case of real estate enforcement proceedings - Portugal, Hungary and Italy. It should be noted that tenders in the electronic system will be available to a wide range of buyers interested in purchasing real estate in enforcement proceedings, which, as can be expected, will promote more participants and achieve higher prices. In addition, this solution will contribute to simplifying and achieving transparency, ensuring a higher level of security for participants in procedural activities, reducing costs and significantly speeding up the process. Digitisation of proceedings due to the situation is a requirement of our time. The introduction of remote hearings and public hearings under current epidemic conditions is a solution to reduce the risk of life and health risks and, if any, to allow the use of any terminal device anywhere, reduces the burden of the participants in the proceedings. Last year showed that in crisis situations such as pandemics, remote work allows both companies and institutions to function properly. Therefore, such a solution should also be applied in the case of justice. On behalf of the Parliamentary Club, Law and Justice I strongly recommend further proceedings over the bill. Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! On behalf of the parliamentary club, I have the honour to make eight amendments that clarify or amend certain wordings. I hope that they will make the legislation a very good law. However, I am surprised that, on the one hand, the Member expresses his concern about the speed of proceedings and, on the other hand, criticizes the solutions to improve this. For this reason, also Mr Suchoń... Mr President, as regards the situation with the concept of "offering," this is not the concept of religious nature in Polish law, it is the concept that has been present in the Polish legal order for more than 80 years because of the fact that "offering" means making an offer. And this concept is present in the Polish legal order, for example in the article. 200 pre-war law on judicial enforcement. This is the President's regulation of 27 October 1932. This concept in the Polish order of civil procedure is still present today, so I am surprised that it arouses your interest. Nevertheless, it is always worth to enrich knowledge also from the history of Polish law. High Izbo! In conclusion, the bill submitted is needed not only because of the pandemic, but also because of the need for the Polish justice system to take another step in digitization. Digitization has progressed dynamically in recent years. This bill will increase the availability of courts, increase the pace of court proceedings, as well as increase the effectiveness of executions, because each of us will be able to participate in auctions without having to go to court. These are very important changes, which is why I ask the High House to adopt this bill. [[The meeting is chaired by Deputy Marshal Małgorzata Gosiewska]]

ParlaMint-PL_2021-04-14-sejm-28-1.u327	Dear Marshal! High Izbo! Dear Minister, I have the honour on behalf of the Parliamentary Club Law and Justice to present a position vis-à-vis the government's draft law on the amendment of the law on local government, the law on district government and the law on local government, print no. 1014, whose primary purpose is to institutionally strengthen youth councils at local government units. A little history to begin with. The idea of establishing youth councils with local self-government units emerged after 1989 and was the result of democratic changes that took place in Poland at the time. As a result, youth councils began to form in many cities. The first such city was Częstochowa, where on 19 September 1990 children's council and youth council were established. This was of a bottom-up nature because there were no legal provisions at the time that would regulate this issue. Such a provision was introduced into the Municipal Government Act in 2001, i.e. after 11 years. This was the article introduced at the time. 5b. Although youth councils were created also in powiats, and youth sejmiki - in voivodships, unfortunately these changes, art. 5b, did not concern these levels of self-government. And so, in fact, we have been in a state of some sort of legal void for 19 years. It is worth noting that art. 5b The law on local government is short and does not say much about youth councils, although according to recent data the problem concerns 514 communes, 63 powiats and 12 voivodships. Therefore, the draft law submitted is expected and needed and should be welcomed. It includes provisions that take into account the functioning of youth councils in the district and voivodship authorities, institutional strengthening of youth councils in the local self-government units and ensuring that youth councils operate more efficiently with the possibility of increasing the impact on local communities. It is worth noting at this point that these intentions of the legislator meet the expectations of youth communities. They have been repeatedly articled, among others. During the Parliamentary Group's deliberations. Supporting Youth Councils in the Units of the Territorial Self-Government, and were included in the analysis entitled: On youth councils prepared in 2018 by the Council of Children and Youth at the Minister of National Education. The amendment of these laws was also recommended by the Council of Dialogue with the Young Generation in its October 2020 resolution. The proposed changes include: clarify the mechanism for setting up youth councils with local self-government units, giving the possibility to submit a request for a council, among others. non-governmental organisations and student and student authorities, the body which will have to respond to such a request, clarify the tasks of youth councils and strengthen their position. The introduction of these necessary and beneficial changes is extremely important. We are still talking about the need to strengthen the civic participation of Polish youth at local level. This will allow for the rules that we will now introduce. This is particularly important when sociological studies show that, unfortunately, most young people are not interested in politics either locally or nationwide. This should be a warning bell for all of us. The proposed changes give the opportunity to increase the level of civic education of young people so that they can gain public and social experience. Young people spoke about the need for legal changes in this area during, among other things, the need for legal changes. of the Permanent Sub-Committee meeting on This was confirmed by the large-scale public consultation of young people, which resulted in many major amendments to the first draft. I am aware that there may be other good proposals that our club will consider and support during the further process of this law. Finally, I would like to point out that the 2019 election programme of Law and Justice included in the chapter "Freedom and social activity" in the sub-section "Objectives" that: there will be an improvement in the institutional position of the youth councils of municipalities by increasing their competence towards greater independence in public life and the obligation to initiate procedures for their creation when this initiative receives significant support in the local community and similar provisions on the creation of youth councils will be introduced into the system of voivodship and district authorities. The project submitted fulfils this obligation. In view of the above, I have the honour to inform the High Sejm that Parliamentary Club Law and Justice will vote in favour [[♪ The bell ♪]] government draft law on amendment of the law on municipal self-government, the law on district self-government and the law on local government. Thank you very much for your attention. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2021-04-14-sejm-28-1.u399	Dear Marshal! High Izbo! I am very pleased that the subject of strengthening youth councils with local self-government units is of such great interest to the parliamentarian state. I am very pleased that the fashion for increasing the participation of young people in public life, which was introduced by the Law and Justice and by the government of Prime Minister Matthew Morawiecki, has taken so much that all parliamentary options have, in the same spirit, expressed themselves and stated that these actions are needed. As we know very well, for many years, young people have had a sense of insufficient influence on decision-making processes and thus insufficient influence on reality. It is clear that, for the reasons we all understand, young people are disproportionately less in decision-making bodies than they are in society. Therefore, institutions should be set up that make it possible to balance this gap and make it possible for young people to have an impact, which is important for decision-making processes. As the Government of Law and Justice, we are very consistently increasing the influence of young people on decision-making processes, which is why we have set up a statutory Council of Dialogue with the Young Generation, which on the one hand has representatives of the President, Prime Minister, Ministers, Local Government, and on the other hand representatives of young people, youth social organisations, youth councils operating at the moment in the municipalities, the Parliament of Polish Students and a whole range of other youth initiatives, which is why in the ministries, cultural institutions, public institutions are set up an advisory group composed of young people, which is why a representative of the government for youth policy has been appointed and therefore, in a participatory manner, in full dialogue with young people, based on consultations in all voivodships, we are preparing a new multi-annual policy strategy of the Polish state for young people. The next step in the same direction is to strengthen youth councils with local self-government units. This is a process that has already begun in the previous parliamentary term. I would like to thank the Parliamentary Group for this. Supporting Youth Councils for Territorial Self-Government Units. The team was founded in the previous term by Mr Kurzepa. He is chaired by Mr Paweł Lisiecki during this term of office. Members of this team began a very serious debate with young people on strengthening youth councils with local self-government units. Conferences were organized, congresses were organized. Young people had the opportunity to present their demands in a very substantive way. These postulates were then presented in the parliamentary group and later in the Council of Dialogue with the Young Generation. ParlaMint-PL_2021-04-14-sejm-28-1.u401	Dear Marshal! High Izbo! On behalf of the Committee on Education, Science and Youth and the Committee on Territorial Government and Regional Policy, I present a report on the Government's draft law amending the Law on Special Solutions related to the prevention, prevention and eradication of COVID-19, other infectious diseases and the crisis situations caused by them, print No 1062. Marshal of the Sejm according to Art. Article 37 (1) (a) and (b) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 1 and Article 1 Article 40 (1) (a) and (b) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 1 of the Rules of Procedure of the Sejm, after consulting the Sejm bureau, sent the above draft law to the Committee on Education, Science and Youth and the Committee on Territorial Government and Regional Policy for its first reading on 29 March 2021. The bill submitted raises three very important issues. Firstly, the reserve of the educational part of the general subsidy is increased by PLN 187 million from the State budget. This appropriation is intended to cover the organisation for pupils in primary and secondary education classes IV-VIII, after their return to schools, of additional activities to support the consolidation of information and skills from selected compulsory general education activities. With regard to amendments to the Public Documents Act due to risks relating to the impossibility of adapting public documents of the first, second and third categories to statutory requirements within the time limits set so far, i.e. by 12 July 2021 for the first and second categories of documents and by 12 July 2023 for the third category of public documents, it is proposed to extend the period for the adjustment by an additional year. Further proposals concern the qualifications of rescue workers and mining paramedics during the epidemic. The project provides for the possibility to include periods of secondment of these staff in hospital assistance to combat epidemics as periods taken into account in determining the right and amount of mining pensions. Thus, periods of secondment are treated equally with underground mining work. It is proposed that these amendments should apply retroactively from 16 May 2020. Five amendments were tabled in the course of the bill's work, of which two were accepted and three rejected amendments were tabled as minority requests. The Commissions: Education, Science and Youth and the Territorial Government and Regional Policy, after first reading and considering this draft law at its meeting on 13 April 2021, ask the High Sejm to approve the attached draft law. At the same time, I would like to inform you that the committees in accordance with Article. Article 43 (1) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 3 The Rules of Procedure of the Sejm shall submit three minority applications at the request of the applicants. Thank you very much. ParlaMint-PL_2021-04-14-sejm-28-1.u483	Good morning. Mrs. Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! I am pleased to present the position of the parliamentary club of the Left in relation to the bill amending the Act on the Charter of the Large Family, the Act on computerization of activities of entities carrying out public tasks and the Act on the care of children under the age of 3. In the explanatory memorandum of the Act, you write that the current government is taking a number of systematic steps to improve the situation of families and citizens, while at the same time seeking to reduce bureaucracy by introducing a number of facilitations both for citizens, businesses and their offices. The harmonisation of public services as part of their digitalisation, which is included in the proposed change, should be a constant direction of government work. And we strongly support that. Today's draft law should be one of the points of digitisation and simplification of procedures. This is also a good time to discuss digital exclusion. The government must ensure that as many people as possible can use public services in electronic form. More than 4.5 million Poles and Poles have never used the Internet, and Poland among the countries of the European Union has one of the highest indicators of people never using the Internet. As the Consumer Federation report shows, the most vulnerable to digital exclusion are households with the lowest incomes and as many as 945 thousand. people with disabilities, i.e. 36% It never used a computer. Dear Government! It's worth paying attention to. At the same time, on the occasion, I would like to point out that, in the proposed amendment, there is no reference to the two problems raised in previous years by both the Ombudsman and the previous Ombudsman. As already indicated in 2014 Marek Michalak, former spokesperson for the rights of the child, the Large Family Card can discriminate against couples without marriage. The basic documents giving entitlement to discounts shall be granted to families who are dependent on at least three children. The problem is that the regulation of 27 May 2014 on detailed conditions for implementing the government multi-child programme narrows down the definition of the family in relation to the Law of 12 March 2004 on social assistance under which it was issued. As a result, the Large Family Card cannot be used e.g. some informal relationships raising up even four children. In a situation where a man and his partner raise four children but do not have a marriage, the father is denied the right, even though he actually runs a common household with his partner and four children. In this connection, I would like to ask: Why do not the rights of the programme have the right of non-formal union holders if the spouse of the parent, where the parent has at least three children, have those rights, even if the spouse does not have any child of his own? The Social Assistance Act provides for a broader definition of the family. They are related or unrelated persons who are in a real relationship, living and managing together. If a similar definition were adopted in the Large Family Charter Act, so-called. Patchwork families would be supported whether or not their parents got married. The rights granted in the Charter of the Large Family are intended not only for adults but also for the youngest members of the family, and children have no influence on the relationship of carers. This means that this regulation is defective. The purpose of the Bill on the Charter of the Big Family is not, as I understand it, to promote the institution of marriage, but to help a family of many children, especially children. The measures are therefore inadequate and may discriminate and therefore need to be amended. Question: "Is this a change in the government's mind?" In addition, in 2016, the Ombudsman warned that children from a child's home or an emergency room could not receive the Large Family Charter. By defining the concept of a family member, the legislator apart from parents and children and the parent's spouse also pointed out foster parents and people running the family home of the child. In this way, however, children were excluded, who were sent to extra-family foster care. Such a restriction is not justified because children do not have an impact on which form of foster care - family care or institutional care - were brought about by the intervention of the care court. The Ombudsman points out that such regulation of the Charter can be accused of breaching the constitutionally guaranteed principle of equality. It is difficult to determine why, in fact, apart from austerity considerations, this limitation is intended. Public authorities are obliged to create a coherent social policy system and to support children and the family, as set out in Article. 72 The basic law guarantees of special assistance and care for unaccompanied children cannot be respected selectively. I'd like to ask if the government is working on these changes. However, since today's proposed bill is intended to introduce a number of changes in ordering and systematic rules for granting the Large Family Charter, in particular as regards the computerisation of these processes, and since we welcome the proposed amendments to the Child Welfare Act under the age of 3, the parliamentary club of the Left will support the proposed proposal in its present form. Thank you very much. [[♪ The bell ♪]]

Could that be the right thing to do? As regards the clarification of the general requirements for childcare under the age of 3, the intention is also to protect our youngest children. It's about checking that these people aren't in the sex offenders' records. Excellent, let's go this way. This project could be summarised in such a way, while talking about pro-family policies cannot be addressed in relation to the tragic demographic situation that is in our homeland. Because the financial system of motivation that we have in Poland today assumes among others. restricting itself to the tool of redistribution. The experience of other European countries shows the high effectiveness of a well-planned system of tax relief progression - growing with each child. Discounts could be calculated either on a monthly basis or on a percentage basis. For many Polish families such motivation to expand the family through really higher earnings would be very important. At the same time, the use of such a relief by nature would be simpler and cheaper than a redistribution that works only at this moment. The Polish system also lacks real family benefits or they are symbolic. The real achievement of these goals probably requires a really thorough and systemic change. A higher tax-free amount would also be important. Another fiscal tool worth paying attention to in the case of family policies is the impact of the State on the prices of certain products, even through VAT. Today, this catalogue of products that have a reduced VAT rate is too narrow. It would be worth expanding to relieve Polish families. Another issue worth discussing would be the question of extending the possibility of caring for children, making it easier because, as we know, not once and not twice the problem in Polish homes is that there is no one to leave children with, especially if both parents work with. Here you might want to consider an extended system that would allow you to open home crèches or home kindergartens, which would be a strong support for today's system that we have. Thank you. ParlaMint-PL_2021-04-14-sejm-28-1.u517	Dear Marshal! High Izbo! Ministers! On 17 March 2021, a draft law amending certain laws on the transfer of funds from open pension funds to individual pension accounts was considered at the plenary meeting of the Sejm. The proposed law provides for the conversion of open pension funds into investment funds that operate individual pension accounts with assets from OFE. Each insured person will have the right to choose, the default option will be to transfer funds from OFE to IKE with the possibility of further supply, but already completely voluntary. At the same time, in the case of persons who decide to move fully to Pillar I, the funds collected in the OFE will be credited to an individual account held in the Social Insurance Institution and the corresponding assets will be transferred to the Demographic Reserve Fund. After the first reading, the law was addressed to the joint committees: Public Finance and Social Policy and Family Policy. The joint committees considered the draft law on 24 March this year. and present a report. The discussion during the committee's work covered a number of aspects. In particular, a 15% fee was discussed at the time of the conversion. During the committee's work, the first request for a public hearing was submitted and the joint committee's request was rejected. Subsequently, a motion to reject the draft law in its entirety was submitted and this request was rejected as well. On the other hand, the Commission adopted amendments concerning, inter alia, speed up the time limit for transfers between OFEs from November to August 2021, supplementing the catalogue of documents necessary for making the transfer payment of declarations of matrimonial property rights, exemption from PIT of funds paid from OFEs to entitled persons and heirs after the loss-making death. An exemption was also introduced from the one-off payment of funds from the OFE by the saver to whom the OFE account was opened in connection with the death of his spouse and many other editorial and legislative amendments. According to tradition, committees also authorised the Legislative Office to make editorial and legislative corrections in the draft law. Finally, the Joint Committees on Public Finance and Social Policy recommend adopting a draft law, simultaneously submitting 11 proposals for minorities that have been submitted, among others. Again, the motion to reject the draft law in its entirety, the proposal to amend the title of the draft law, etc. The report submitted to the committee is contained in print No 1035. The committees recommend the adoption of this law. Thank you very much.
ParlaMint-PL_2021-04-14-sejm-28-1.u518	Thank you, sir. The Sejm agreed that in the discussion on this item it would hear five minutes' statements on behalf of clubs and circles. I'm opening a discussion. Mr Adam Sněžek, Parliamentary Club Law and Justice, will be the first to speak. You're welcome.
ParlaMint-PL_2021-04-14-sejm-28-1.u519	Mrs. Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! The Public Finance and Social Policy and Family Committees discussed the government print project No. 1005 at the joint meeting and, having regard to the amendments, adopted a draft law amending certain laws in connection with the transfer of funds from open pension funds to individual pension accounts, print No. 1035. This project aims to repair the pension system strained by the large and unexpected transfer of assets from OFE to ZUS by the Government of the Civic Platform and the Polish People's Party in 2014 for the rescue of mismanaged state finances. The solutions adopted in the draft law will be used to rebuild the capital pillar on new principles while guaranteeing the private nature of the measures collected under it. The proposed law provides for the conversion of open pension funds into investment funds operating individual pension accounts with assets from OFE. The insured will decide for themselves to transfer their funds raised in open funds from contributions to the IKE or to the ZUS. Persons who choose the complete transition to Pillar I through the declaration will have transferred their assets from OFE to the Demographic Reserve Fund and their value will be recorded in an individual account held in the ZUS. The default option will be to transfer funds to the IKE with the possibility of further voluntary power supply. The contributions collected on individual pension accounts will be private property that saves inviolable property from the State. The payment of these measures after the insured person's retirement age will be tax-free. The equivalent of income tax and health insurance contributions charged to pensioners from the Social Insurance Fund will be a one-off conversion fee of 15% for funds transferred to IKE. . Individual pension accounts combined with developing employee capital plans will constitute a stronger second pillar of the pension system. These solutions which increase the opportunities and guarantees of voluntary retirement savings will strengthen citizens' confidence in the pension system and stabilise the pension security of Poles. This will mean an increase in long-term private savings, which will positively affect the economic stability of the country, strengthening the Polish capital market and its investment potential. Parliamentary Club Law and Justice will vote in favour of the bill submitted. At the same time, our club makes six amendments clarifying certain provisions of the Act. Thank you for your attention. ParlaMint-PL_2021-04-14-sejm-28-1.u576	Thank you very much, Marshal. High Izbo! Clarify the provisions, starting with the scope of this law in Article. 1, 2, 3 and 4, introducing new regulations such as booking agreements, new information obligations, i.e. the obligation to deliver the information prospectus to the purchaser, and to clarify the provisions on the booking fee and the conditions for its reimbursement - I would remind you that it will not be able to exceed 1% the price of the premises or house given in the information prospectus - all this serves to protect the interests of the buyer. The provisions on the development agreement have been clarified and harmonised. The subject matter of this agreement may be the transfer of ownership of a residential establishment, a family house or a commercial establishment purchased with it. The developer may also conclude agreements with the buyer referred to in Article. 2 (2) Points 1, 2, 3 and 5, together with contracts the object of which is ownership or a fraction of the ownership of the commercial premises. The claims arising from these agreements shall be entered in the perpetual book. Clearly, there is a strengthening of the position of the buyer of the premises or of the house. A completely new solution is the Developer Guarantee Fund. Regardless of the type of measures taken to protect the buyer's payments - whether we are talking about a closed residential trust account or an open residential trust account - the developer will be obliged to pay contributions to the Developer Guarantee Fund. The Developer Guarantee Fund is a separate account with the Insurance Guarantee Fund. The guarantee of payment is covered by all contract of the developer with the buyer. The DFG guarantee will also be triggered in the event of bankruptcy of the bank holding the residential trust account and therefore the buyer's funds paid into the residential trust account will be 100% protected. The proposed amount of the DFG contribution is 2% for open housing accounts and 0,2% for closed accounts. High Izbo! This project contains provisions that clarify the acceptance procedure, which allow for the possibility of refusing to receive the defective premises and introducing an obligation to remove defects within the prescribed time limits. However, the law should strike a balance between the burdens, rights and obligations of both parties to the agreement. It is not appropriate to say that one party to the contract, in this case the developer, bears the costs of expertise even in case of non-confirmation of a material defect. Hence the amendment to the article. Article 40 (1) (a) and (b) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 16 giving a new sound to this paragraph. In accordance with the 1st amendment of the package of amendments I submit on behalf of the club, paragraph. 16 will read as follows: In the event that the construction assessor has not established the existence of a material defect, the costs of the construction assessor's opinion shall be borne entirely by the purchaser. High Izbo! The matter has been discussed several times: and at the subcommittee meeting, and at the committee meeting. Therefore, as announced, we are making an amendment. She argued to the public that there was no justification for charging the developer with the costs of an expert's opinion when the assessor found no significant defect. It is not justified to charge only one party, in each case - either in whole or in part, as so far as it is in the provisions of the project - the cost of the opinion is borne by the developer. The adoption of this amendment will also clearly serve to eliminate cases of possible abuse by purchasers of their subjective rights and to raise unjustified allegations in the course of receipt, and I believe that the ratio legis of this law will not be affected. ParlaMint-PL_2021-04-14-sejm-28-1.u609	Mr. Marshal! Minister! High Seym! I have the honour to present the position of the Parliamentary Club Law and Justice towards the government draft law amending the Law on the provision of health care financed by public funds and certain other laws, print No. 1013. The bill presented above all is an amendment to the Law of 27 August 2004 on public-funded healthcare provision. It changes the procedure for mapping health needs and introduces transformation plans - national and voivodship - in place of priorities for regional health policy. The aim of this regulation will be to improve the strategic planning system for health protection. The proposed changes centralise the management by giving the Minister of Health the power to draw up a map of health needs, develop a national transformation plan and evaluate and approve voivodship transformation plans. They also introduce powers for the President of the National Health Fund to draw up a draft plan for the purchase of health care services in the area of the voivodship. Formerly, it was done by the directors of the NFZ Voivodeship Branches. The map is to be valid for 7 years, the first in 6 years from 1 January 2022 to 31 December 2027, as are the transformation plans, with the possibility of being modified in line with updated data after 4 years. The Health Need Map will be a key document to identify health needs and challenges for the organisation of the healthcare system. It will be the foundation of the creation of a national transformation plan. The national transition plan will be developed, established, monitored and updated by the Minister of Health. The advisory vote will be held by the Social Dialogue Council, President of the NFZ, President of the Agency for Medical Technology and Tarification, Director of the State Hygiene Plant. Voivodship transformation plans will be drawn up by the voivodships and the consultative vote will have voivodship councils on health needs. They will be composed, apart from the voivodeship: representatives of the voivodeship, representatives of the President of the NFZ, representative of the President of the Agency for Medical Technology and Tarification, representative of the Marshal of the voivodship, representative of the State Provincial Sanitary Inspector, representative of the patient's attorney, representative of the Director of the National Institute of Public Health - State Hygiene Institute, representative of the Minister responsible for Health. The draft law aims primarily at introducing organisational changes to improve the operation of the system in order to ensure equal access to public funded healthcare services. Health protection in Poland does not work well despite much more financial resources in the system. Directional changes are needed. And this law can be the basis for the implementation of directional changes. It does not refer to specific methods of diagnosis, treatment, but sets out how the necessary changes are to be made so as to ensure equal access to public-funded healthcare for all. Patients must be provided with a universal and efficient comprehensive health care system that facilitates daily life. It is important to cooperate with medical providers at all levels: family doctor, rescue system, county hospitals, voivodship and clinical hospitals. The transformation plans that have been developed can give this an orderly character. On behalf of the Parliamentary Club, I recommend the Law and Justice to proceed with the proposed bill. Thank you.

ParlaMint-PL_2021-04-14-sejm-28-1.u636	Thank you very much. Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! I have the honour to present the position of the Law and Justice Club vis-à-vis the government's draft law amending the National Debt Register Act and some other laws. The bill is designed in addition to the basic amendment, i.e. amendments to the Act on the National Debt Register, also amends the Act - Bankruptcy Law and the Law - Restructuring Law, are such major changes, while also affects the Act on Judicial Costs in Civil Matters, the Act on the License of Restructuring Advisor and the Act on the National Court Register. There is no doubt that, given the growing number of cases dealt with by common courts, including bankruptcy and restructuring cases, it is necessary to adopt legislative and IT arrangements that will enable these proceedings to be managed efficiently, ensuring that citizens are able to exercise their right to court. Ensuring the capacity of the general court requires full information on civil proceedings. This information makes it possible to speed up and streamline proceedings, better inform the parties and the participants in proceedings, reduce the costs of judicial proceedings and the overall costs of the judiciary, increase the transparency of these proceedings and increase the capacity to supervise the activities of the courts. The fact that measures have been taken in the Ministry of Justice to inform proceedings should be welcomed. To date, the widest scope of informationization of court proceedings is provided for by the Act of 6 December 2018 on the National Debt Register. Important solutions there can be the beginning of full informationalisation of the judiciary, especially since restructuring and organisational insolvency proceedings are the most difficult legal proceedings. This is because there are two judicial authorities, i.e. the court and the judge-counsel, the out-of-court authorities: the administrator, the overseer, the administrator, the debtor, there are creditors, sometimes even a thousand and more. In restructuring proceedings, insolvency proceedings are carried out which do not occur in other proceedings. The procedural documents and documents as a result of this law will be created and submitted using forms made available in the electronic system by the Minister of Justice. This will apply to a number of documents, including inventory and lists of claims, inventory, distribution plans. The assumption is that the ICT system will not only be a platform for posting letters and documents, but will also be a system in which data will be processed in such a way as to improve the management of the bankruptcy and restructuring case. The solutions set out in the National Debt Register Act therefore cover the full range of processes necessary to inform proceedings, i.e. the introduction of the principle of electronic communication with the court, the introduction of electronic service, the introduction of the principle of electronic decision-making, the introduction of solutions to protect the rights of digitally excluded persons, the introduction of electronic files and the provision of access to files via the Internet and the secretariats of the court. Let me remind you that 7781 consumer insolvencys were recorded in 2019. Assuming that about 10 different types of documents are created in each case, due to the entry into force of the proposed solutions, the electronicisation will cover around 77 thousand. documents arising from consumer insolvency cases. And that's the most important thing in this bill. As I said, in the field of insolvency law, there are many changes in which the protection proceedings depart from the horizontal complaint made in the Code of Civil Procedure for the provision of collateral, there is provision for clarification of the rules on complaints by a precise indication in which cases the court of second instance considers the complaint and in which that restructuring court in another composition. As far as transparency is concerned, the proposal also envisages introducing (...) a solution consisting of the possibility of self-checking an earlier decision as a result of the complaint. As a general rule, these changes result from the technical solutions used in the construction of the National Debt Register and the IT system for judicial proceedings. Their aim is to ensure coherence between technical regulations and legislation. At the same time, Mr Marshal, I would like to make amendments to this project: three amendments on insolvency law and one amendment on the National Debt Register. The Law and Justice Club... On 16 March 2020, the European Commission asked Member States to inform it by 30 June of the body which Member States have designated or intend to designate as a single liaison office. ParlaMint-PL_2021-04-14-sejm-28-1.u660	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! On behalf of the Civic Coalition Club, I have the honour to present my position on the report of the Committee on Economics and Development on the Government's draft law amending the Law on conformity assessment and market surveillance systems, print no. 1079. Control of products subject to conformity assessment is a very important process for both consumers and traders. One of its main objectives is to ensure that only products meeting certain requirements are placed on the Union market, regardless of where they were manufactured. This protects consumers from products that may pose a risk to life, health or the environment. The conformity assessment system also protects the interests of businesses, restricts unfair competition, is one of the cornerstones of the common market. Uniform product requirements and a common market surveillance system provide the basis for the free movement of goods. The mechanism for protecting consumers and businesses is still being improved. That Regulation of the European Parliament and of the Council of 20 June 2019 on market surveillance and product conformity has already established a European Product Compliance Network, to which the single liaison offices designated by the Member States will be members. The essence of the submitted project is the identification of the entity that will represent our country in the mentioned network. On 26 April this year. The government inter-ministerial team for the reform of conformity assessment and market surveillance systems has adopted a recommendation that the tasks of a single liaison office in Poland should be entrusted to the President of the Office for Competition and Consumer Protection as the most appropriate in view of the role he plays in the national market surveillance system and the experience and competence gained in carrying out tasks at European level. It provides the best guarantee that the national market surveillance strategy required by that Regulation is to be drawn up. The Committee on Economic Affairs and Development considered this draft at its meeting on 13 April. The amendments adopted were legislative in nature, not controversial. When asked to secure funding for the additional tasks of the President of the OOKiK, I heard that they would be carried out within the framework of the Office's resources, without additional costs for the state budget. The Commission supported the proposed proposal by a majority. On behalf of the Civic Coalition Club, I request that her report be adopted. Thank you for your attention.
